The episode has since sparked intense discussion among artists , politicians and commentators regarding freedom of speech , the responsibilities of public officials , and whether major film festivals should remain strictly cultural or continue to serve as forums for global political advocacy . <p> Festival organisers have so far avoided announcing punitive measures , but insiders suggest the incident is likely to influence future discussions on stage protocols , political statements and security planning at high-profile international events . 
@@134779041 <h> Israel responsible for two-thirds of 2025 media worker deaths : CPJ <p> The Committee to Protect Journalists ( CPJ ) has documented a second consecutive year of record fatalities , driven largely by Israel 's ongoing assault on Gaza and what it calls an unprecedented campaign against the press . <p> The New York-based media freedom watchdog says more than 60% of the 86 journalists killed by Israeli fire in 2025 were Palestinian reporters covering the war from inside the war-torn enclave , where UN experts and human rights organisations have described the scale and pattern of violence as amounting to genocide . <p> According to the report , more than three-quarters of journalists killed last year died in conflict zones . Ukraine saw four deaths and Sudan nine , only slightly higher than in 2024 . Gaza , by contrast , still bore far heavier losses , with Israel responsible for the majority . Since the group began tracking targeted killings of media workers three decades ago , the Israel Defense Forces have been responsible for more such deaths than any other state military . <p> In its @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ zones . According to the press freedom group , India , often described as the world 's largest democracy , loses at least one journalist to work-related killings each year , a pattern mirrored in Mexico over the past decade . <p> In Mexico , at least six journalists were killed last year , up from five in 2024 and two in 2023 . All six , the CPJ notes , are unsolved , continuing a longstanding pattern of journalists ' killers going undiscovered and unprosecuted due to powerful criminal influence over police and political activity , and widespread corruption . At least one journalist has been killed in Bangladesh and Colombia -- as well as by Israel -- every year for the past five years . <p> Saudi Arabia and Pakistan are also featured in the grim tally of press deaths worldwide . Both countries suffer from weak rule of law , allowing criminal factions to operate with impunity and political leaders to exercise unchecked power , the advocacy group cautioned . <p> In countries with weaker democracies , journalists have been brutally targeted for reporting on corruption and @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ saw reporters meet tragic and mysterious ends . Bangladeshi journalist Asaduzzaman Tuhin , the CPJ reports , was chased and hacked to death by armed assailants in a killing orchestrated by a fraud ring , according to police . <p> Tuhin 's employer , the Bangla-language daily Protidiner Kagoj , said the attack came after he filmed several armed men assaulting a man in a public dispute . In India , the mutilated body of freelance journalist Mukesh Chandrakar was found in a septic tank weeks after NDTV aired his investigation into alleged corruption in a 1.2 billion rupee ( $12 million ) road project . <p> Similarly , in the Philippines , which also has a long history of violence against journalists , three journalists were shot dead , including veteran publisher Juan Dayang . So far , only one case has resulted in an arrest . <p> Drone killings <p> In its report , the CPJ noted a sharp rise in journalist deaths caused by drones , unmanned aircraft , and other remotely controlled devices in 2025 . Killings of press members attributed to such attacks surged from @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ drone-related deaths -- to 39 last year . Military drones , the advocacy group says , were confirmed or believed to be responsible for 33 of these fatalities . <p> Of those killed by drones in 2025 , 28 were struck by Israel 's military in Gaza , five by Sudan 's paramilitary Rapid Support Forces , four by Russia in Ukraine , one by Houthi forces in Yemen , and one by a suspected Turkish strike in Iraq . Over the past three years , CPJ records a total of 62 journalists killed in drone attacks , with Israel responsible for three-quarters of those deaths between 2023 and 2025 . <p> Paying for the truth <p> Journalists , the CPJ notes , are particularly vulnerable in countries where conflict continues , corrupt politics prevails , and authoritarian regimes weaken the rule of law . <p> The report adds that declines in press freedom -- including the closure of independent outlets , censorship , and physical attacks on media , including killings -- often serve as early indicators of democratic erosion . <p> Globally , the press watchdog observes , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ those who kill journalists , allowing them to evade justice year after year . <p> Silenced by smear <p> The media advocacy group warns of a growing pattern in which unsubstantiated allegations of criminal activity are used to justify attacks on the press . This trend appears both in the large numbers of journalists detained for their work and in the rationales offered for their killings . <p> Israel , the CPJ notes , has repeatedly killed journalists whom it later -- or in some cases preemptively -- claimed were militants , without providing credible evidence . The most striking example of this , the advocacy group says , is the targeting of Al Jazeera reporter Anas al-Sharif , who had publicly warned that his life was at risk after repeated , unverified smears by Israeli authorities . <p> To date , the press freedom group states in its report that no one has been held accountable for any targeted killing of a journalist by Israel since October 7 , 2023 , or in the preceding 22 years . <p> Call for protection <p> The continued failure of government leaders @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ New York -- based group cautioned , lays the groundwork for further killings -- even in countries that are not at war . Media practitioners , it notes , were killed in Mexico , India , and the Philippines in 2025 , all nations that have persistently failed to secure justice for journalists ' murders . <p> In its report , the CPJ called for radical reform of how governments investigate journalist killings , including the establishment of an international investigative task force and the imposition of targeted sanctions . <p> " The uptick in journalist killings is symptomatic of a wider decline in press freedom and journalist safety globally , " the group warned , adding that a near-record number of media workers were jailed in 2025 amid smear campaigns and legal abuses aimed at criminalizing reporting . <p> CPJ also reports a growing wave of online harassment and physical attacks on journalists , driven by increasingly hostile rhetoric toward the press -- even in countries that claim democratic credentials .

These actions are widely considered illegal under international law . Israel 's expansion of control over Area C of the West Bank reflects a broader strategy to consolidate settlement dominance and restrict Palestinian autonomy . Benjamin Netanyahu 's government has unilaterally exercised control over Area C -- 60% of the territory under complete Israeli military and civil authority . <p> Israel 's occupation &amp; expansion trajectory : According to Peace Now , an Israeli anti-settlement watchdog , Israel has seized more than 50 square kilometers ( 19.3 square miles ) of Palestinian land since 1998 . This expansion primarily involves the construction of Israeli settlements in the occupied West Bank and East Jerusalem , areas under long-standing dispute . While broader historical expropriations since 1948 amounted to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ consolidation through settlement growth and land confiscation , often accompanied by legal and administrative measures that limit Palestinian land use . <p> By expanding Jewish settlements , enforcing land registration laws and restricting Palestinian development , Israel has systematically altered the demographic and legal landscape . These actions , though not constituting formal annexation , mirror its de facto control , undermining Palestinian sovereignty and international law . The lack of UN recognition and ongoing occupation underscore the contested status of Area C. Recent decisions by Israeli authorities , described by Palestinian groups as ' fascist settler-colonial , ' aim to annex territory and enforce military-administrative rule . <p> These moves undermine international law and peace efforts , intensifying tensions . The designation of Area C for exclusive Israeli control limits Palestinian development and land use , exacerbating humanitarian conditions . A central mechanism involves an administrative decision to restart the land registration process in Area C for the first time since 1967 . This process allows for land that is unregistered or where ownership can not be formally proven by Palestinians ( often due to lost or destroyed documents @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ used for Israeli settlements . Israel has continuously expanded existing settlements and established new , often unauthorized , outposts which are later retroactively legalized . These settlements are strategically placed to fragment the West Bank and cut off Palestinian population centers from East Jerusalem . This erases the legal distinction between the West Bank and Israel proper for Israeli settlers , while Palestinians remain under military land , Israel imposes a restrictive planning regime where it is nearly impossible for Palestinians to obtain building permits in Area C. <p> Notably , Palestinian structures built without permits are frequently demolished , leading to displacement , while settlement construction is streamlined . By implementing measures that facilitate annexation and limit legal oversight , his government has fostered a " state of exception " where domestic law supersedes international legal norms . <p> To achieve its ulterior motives , Israeli Government pursued a massive judicial overhaul to limit the Supreme Court 's power , such as removing its ability to void government decisions based on the " reasonableness " standard . And particularly the 2023 law weakening the Supreme Court 's power to invalidate laws -- effectively limits judicial review , enabling the Knesset to pass legislation with minimal oversight . An independent judiciary is a key defence against international prosecution ( the principle of complementarity ) ; by weakening it , the government risks removing the legal " shield " that prevents bodies @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ in Israeli affairs . <p> Netanya 's government passed laws to ban UNRWA operations within Israeli-controlled territory . This move was rejected by the International Court of Justice ( ICJ ) , which affirmed Israel 's obligation to allow UN aid into Gaza despite domestic bans . To bypass international prohibitions on acquiring territory by force , the government introduced domestic legal mechanisms like " Protection of Antiquities and Environment " to justify enforcement and demolitions in Areas A and B of the West Bank -- zones originally under Palestinian control per the Oslo Accords . This shift undermines checks on executive and legislative authority , potentially allowing Israel to enact laws inconsistent with international legal norms without meaningful recourse . It is difficult to view it as coincidence that less than 24 hours after Modi returned to India , the United States and Israel launched coordinated strikes on Iran . <p> This was not the first instance of such alignment . In June 2025 , during an earlier Israeli attack on Iran , India was accused of playing a similar role by providing human intelligence and material support through Afghanistan . At that time , Indian intelligence networks operating inside Iran under the cover of business and employment were reportedly activated to assist Israeli @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ -- Israel assault while Israel , through its intelligence and proxy footprint , is effectively positioned along Iran 's eastern flank -- in Afghanistan . <p> Pakistan , which shares borders with Iran , Afghanistan , and India , finds itself in an exceptionally precarious position . It is a weather-beaten ally of the United States and increasingly encircled by hostile actors . In recent months , Pakistan has already experienced war-like situations on both its eastern and western borders -- with India and Afghanistan respectively . <p> Any overt military or strategic support by Pakistan for Iran against the United States could invite catastrophic consequences . Yet Islamabad also fears that it may itself become the next target . Netanyahu 's repeated references to a " free Balochistan " are deeply alarming , particularly given credible reports that since 2020 Israel has funded separatist narratives in Balochistan through multiple think tanks and non-governmental organizations . <p> There is now little doubt that Israel has activated two strategic proxies in South Asia to paralyze the region : India in the east and the Taliban regime in the west . The nexus of India , Israel , the United States , and Afghanistan effectively ensured that Pakistan , the world 's fifth-largest military and Iran 's most vocal regional supporter becomes preoccupied with internal security and border defence , limiting its ability to stand firmly with Tehran . During previous periods of heightened regional tensions , Pakistan 's diplomatic influence and military deterrence reportedly altered the strategic calculations of key actors . According to the narrative , the strategy was subsequently revised . <p> Following recent high-level discussions and closely coordinated strategic alignments between Indian Prime Minister Narendra Modi and the Israeli leadership during Modi 's visit to Israel , a new framework was allegedly finalized . Sources claim that kinetic strikes against Iran were approved only after a large-scale diversion was created elsewhere in the region . <p> Master Proxy <p> The reported strategy involved escalating tensions on the Afghan front by @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ " to entangle Pakistan , thereby limiting Islamabad 's capacity to respond diplomatically and militarily . Once Pakistan was preoccupied with multiple security challenges , attention was shifted toward Iran . Analysts describe the approach as involving different theatres but following a similar operational script . <p> Phase One : Indo-Israeli Distraction Targeting Pakistan <p> According to the assessment , isolating Iran required first constraining Pakistan . India 's objective of maintaining sustained pressure on Pakistan was seen as converging with Israel 's interest in weakening Iran 's regional support structure . In this context , the Afghan Taliban ( TTA ) were allegedly positioned as a large-scale proxy force . <p> After what is described as an Indo-Israeli consensus , the approximately 2,600-kilometre Durand Line reportedly witnessed a sharp escalation . Proxy groups allegedly carried out hundreds of mass-casualty attacks against Pakistani security forces . This surge in violence forced Pakistan to divert significant military resources and diplomatic focus toward its western border , limiting its ability to engage effectively in broader regional peace efforts . As a result , Pakistan was reportedly constrained , reducing Iran 's access @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ Eastern Pressure on Iran <p> With Pakistan heavily engaged on its western frontier , the same proxy dynamics were allegedly redirected toward Iran 's roughly 900-kilometre eastern border . The Afghan Taliban are accused of exerting economic and geographic pressure on Iran through multiple means . <p> One such measure involved water resources . The TTA is alleged to have violated the 1973 Helmand River Treaty by sharply restricting Iran 's legally entitled share of 820 million cubic metres of water to about four percent , exacerbating instability in Iran 's Sistan-Baluchestan province and contributing to environmental degradation and social unrest . <p> In parallel , the porous border allegedly became a launchpad for armed Sunni militant groups , including Jaish al-Adl . These groups reportedly forced Iran 's Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps ( IRGC ) to commit substantial manpower and resources to counter persistent militant activity . The terrorists are heavily armed , with access to an estimated $7 billion worth of leftover US military equipment , intensifying pressure on Iran 's eastern security apparatus . <p> What unfolds next remains uncertain .
